Branch resolves at init, every TurnEnd, and run end (an unborn branch — `git init`, no commit yet — falls back to `git symbolic-ref --short HEAD`). Context `%` = `agent.Status().ContextTokens` (usage-anchored, §6) vs the catalog window. Shown as a whole number from 10% up, with one decimal below (`0.4%`; `<0.1%` for a sliver) so a small conversation in a 1M window does not read as a stuck `0%`; `in/out` are the session's cumulative input tokens (fresh + cache reads + cache writes) and output tokens.
- **Keys**: `enter` send · `shift+enter`/`alt+enter`/`ctrl+j` newline (a muted hint is printed **once per machine** — `hints.json` in the data dir — when the terminal reports no key disambiguation, and the placeholder then names `ctrl+j`; the view requests `ReportAllKeysAsEscapeCodes` **and** `ReportAlternateKeys` — with all keys reported as escape codes the terminal sends the *unshifted* codepoint plus a shift modifier, so without the second flag a shifted symbol loses its shift (`shift+/` arrived as `/`; the alternate-key form `CSI 47:63;2u` carries the shifted codepoint, pinned by `TestShiftedSymbolReachesTheDraft` / `TestViewRequestsAlternateKeys`) · `↑/↓` history at single-line buffer edges (in the `/`-dropdown: move its selection) · `shift+tab` **cycle the permission mode** (v0.4.0-beta): `default → auto → plan → yolo → default` (rev 26: plan rides only on the default mode — the plan stop is entered from auto and lands at `default`, toggling the plan flag; the stop after plan is yolo, which turns plan off first, and yolo wraps back to `default`); refused mid-run like `/mode` (`refuseRunning`), and plan/mode are applied in an order that never passes through an unenforced state (entering plan or a wider mode drops the other side first — the agent's `SetPlan`/`SetMode` do it, so every path obeys it) (pinned: `TestNextMode`, `TestShiftTabCyclesMode`, `TestShiftTabRefusedWhileRunning`) · `ctrl+t` **cycle the effort level** over the current model's supported levels, wrapping (effort moved here from the old shift+tab; `/effort` unchanged; refused mid-run; models with fewer than 2 levels print a muted note) (pinned: `TestCtrlTCyclesEffort`) · `esc` interrupt (approval visible → deny; `/`-dropdown open → dismiss it) · `ctrl+c` clear input / double-press ≤1s on empty quits (the first press shows the hint row; also inside the pager) · `ctrl+o` pager on the latest item (on the pending approval's whole command while a question is shown) · `alt+t` pager on the latest thinking block (printed thinking lines end with `· alt+t to read`; `/show <n>` opens any item) · `alt+p` toggle paste chips · `g`/`G`/`q`/`esc`/arrows/pgup/pgdn in the pager (alt-screen, soft-wrapped, closed by `q`/`esc`; its title row is clamped to one row and shows the scroll position `N% · q to close`). **Exit**: the final frame keeps the rule and the status bar (the input box and hints are dropped), and when the session holds anything (a run, a `!` note, a resume) `session <id8> · resume with: moca --resume <id8>` prints below it.
- **Pager**: scrollback output produced while the alt-screen pager is open is held (Bubble Tea's `Println` is lost in the alt screen) and printed shortly after it closes — a `releaseMsg` ~50 ms later, once the renderer has left the alt screen (a `Println` that beats the exit is written to the alt screen and lost).
- **Input**: pastes insert at the cursor (CR/CRLF normalized to LF); chips `[paste N lines #K]` for >50-line or control-byte pastes (content verbatim until send; unsafe pastes never expand); a broken marker sends literally; history stores expanded text and re-chips control-byte entries on recall; `esc` returns queued steering above the draft.
- **Runs**: numbered items shared across tools and thinking (`▸ #7 tool summary`, `✗` on error — the mark in red, `⋯ #6 thinking N lines · 12s` — `1 line` singular; bodies open in the pager); a thinking block's item line prints when the block **ends** — before the response text or tool item that follows it, never after — carrying the duration it streamed (`· <1s` under a second; omitted for a block not accumulated from deltas); streaming text commits completed lines to scrollback; the activity row shows the running tool (`<name>…`) or `thinking…`; thinking is never expanded outside the pager. A message sent mid-run is **steering** (queued, drained after the current tool batch; printed `↳ queued:` / `↳ sent:`); a no-tool-call turn appends it and continues.
- **`--model provider/model`** overrides the config's model for the process. It is applied **before validation** and adds a local provider it names (`--model ollama/qwen3` is the opt-in for a provider with no config entry — validating first rejected the model the user asked for, F-1).
- **Commands**: `/model [id]` (no argument: a modal picker over the **active** providers' models — §6, `ActiveModels`: a stored key, a resolvable `env:` reference or a configured local server — current model preselected and always listed, marked `no key (/login …)` when its provider went inactive; with an id: switch; the switch is refused while running — a plain listing is printed instead; forfeits the prompt cache), `/clear-models-cache` (v0.7.10: deletes every provider's cached model list (`<data>/models-cache/`, §4) and re-reads the **current** model's provider at once — off the event loop, 6 s cap, `re-reading <provider> models…` in the status line; the scrollback line reads `models cache cleared: N providers, M models`, then `— <provider> re-read now (K models)`, or a warning naming a re-read that failed (that provider's rows stay as they were); `the others refetch on the next /model`; refused while a run or a `!` is in progress), `/resume [id8|last]` (a picker over this directory's other sessions — id · age · first user line — or `/resume <id8>`, which refuses an id belonging to another directory; `/resume last` resumes this directory's newest *other* session — the open one is skipped — or says `no other sessions for this directory`; the stored model/effort win as with `--resume`; the open session is untouched if the target cannot be resumed; refused while busy), `/sessions` (the session manager: a picker over this directory's stored sessions, newest first, at most 100 rows — the title reads `newest 100 of N` beyond that; the open one included and marked `(current)`, message-less ones shown as `(no messages)` — enter switches to one (the `/resume` rules), `ctrl+d` asks `delete session <id8>? [y] delete · [n] back` and on `y` unlinks the file through the single-writer lock (`session.Delete`; a session held open by another moca process → `open in another moca process`, nothing removed), then reopens the panel minus the row; the open session refuses deletion — `/clear` starts a new one; refused while busy; no sessions → `no sessions for this directory`), `/effort [level]` (also `ctrl+t`: the next supported level, wrapping; refused where `/effort` is), `/hard` (modelHard + high ↔ saved pair), `/yolo` (between runs; red bar field; shorthand for `SetMode(yolo)`/`SetMode(default)` — one `permission_mode` entry + one `ModeChanged` notice either way; entering yolo with plan on turns plan off first: a `PlanChanged` notice and entry, then the mode's), `/mode [default|auto|yolo|plan]` (v0.4.0-beta; rev 27: the same four words as `--mode` — no argument prints the current state and the shift+tab cycle; an argument sets it — refused while a run or `!` is in progress, like `/yolo`, before the name is even validated; `/mode plan` enters the plan scope via `SetPlan(true)`, landing at `default` even from a wider mode; `/mode default` exits it; `/mode auto|yolo` turn it off through `SetMode` — plan rides only on default) (pinned: `TestModeCommandShowsAndSets`, `TestModeCommandPlanSymmetry`), `/plan [request]` (rev 20: toggle plan mode — runs write an implementation plan to `docs/plans/<slug>.md` with every write outside `docs/plans/*.md` refused (shell, mcp and web stay available under the normal rules); a request argument plans it immediately; between runs; turning plan on in auto/yolo leaves the mode for `default` first (a `ModeChanged` notice and entry, then `PlanChanged`) — plan rides only on default, rev 26; blue `Plan` bar field), `/execute <plan.md> [instructions]` (rev 21: execute an implementation plan step by step — `Agent.RunPlan`; the earlier `/do` and `/implement` names are gone, with no alias; refused while running and in plan mode), `/clear` (new session file; old stays resumable; keeps the current model/effort — hard mode exits to its saved pair — and the commands approved with `[a]`; yolo resets to the startup flag), `/compact` (manual compaction: runs `agent.Compact` off the event loop on a cancellable context — `esc` or quit cancels it and prints `compaction cancelled` — with a `compacting…` transient; `nothing to compact` when the live window fits `keepRecentTokens`; prints `⋯ compacted: N → M tokens` on success; a run, `!`, `/clear`, `/model`, `/effort`, `/hard`, `/yolo` and another `/compact` are refused while it is in flight), `/cost` (`in T (fresh F · cache read R · cache write W) · out O · $cost`, token counts in the bar's compact form; `subscription` for OAuth), `/undo`, `/copy` (OSC 52 — the last assistant text; a final turn without text keeps the previous one), `/show <n>`, **`/remember <text>`** (v0.7.0, §12: hands the run's own text — never a prompt template's body — to the agent as the user's rule to record; refused mid-run like `/execute`), `/login [provider]`, `/logout [provider]`, `/help`, `/exit` (`/q`, `/quit` aliases — quits, unless a prompt template already has that name; an in-flight run/compaction is cancelled and recorded, same as `ctrl+c` twice); `/model`,`/clear-models-cache`,`/effort`,`/hard`,`/yolo`,`/plan`,`/execute`,`/clear`,`/login`,`/logout` are refused **while a run or a `!` command is in progress**. Prompt templates: `/name args` with `$ARGUMENTS`/`$@`/`$1..$9`/`$$`; built-in names win, then prompt templates, then skills (§12 — `/create-command`, `self-knowledge` and every discovered skill are invokable this way); unknown → error. **`create-command` is an embedded built-in skill** (rev 27 — nothing is seeded into the config dir; a previously seeded `create-command.md` is user-owned and keeps winning over the skill): running it has the agent create or update a saved command — personal ones in the global prompts dir (a write outside the workdir, so moca asks for approval) or project ones under `.moca/prompts/`. The prompt dirs and the agent's skill list are re-read when a run or `!` command finishes (`AppOptions.PromptDirs`, `Agent.ReloadSkills`), so a command or skill written during a run is usable without a restart. `/`-escaped text: `//x` sends `/x`. A submitted command **echoes into the scrollback as a user message** (the same `›` band, blank line included) before it runs, so the transcript reads as everything the user sent — refusals and errors follow the echo; `/exit` quits after the echo lands.
- **`/`-dropdown** (autocomplete): while the draft is exactly `/<word>` (no space/newline yet, not the `//` escape) the live-region slot above the input rule opens with the panel's grey rule (v0.7.5) and lists the built-in commands plus the loaded prompt templates (tagged `prompt: `) and the agent's discovered skills (tagged `skill: `; rev 27) — **not** the project's auto skills (rev 33: a learned skill is not a command; `/help` prints them on one `auto skills:` line), prefix-filtered case-insensitively as the word grows (`/lo` → `login`, `logout`; built-ins win a name collision, then prompts over skills, and a shadowed name is listed once). Rows are `❯ name  hint` (cursor row marked, hint dim — the tag rides the muted hint region — clamped to the width) — at most 8 rows (fewer on short terminals), the window follows the cursor, and the legend shows `n/N · ↑/↓ select · tab complete · esc dismiss` when the list overflows. `↑`/`↓` move the selection, `tab` completes the selection into the draft (trailing space, cursor parked at the end), `enter` completes a partial word and submits an exact name (the pre-dropdown behaviour), `esc` dismisses until the word changes; no matches hides the panel, as does a run/`!`/compaction/login wizard/pager/approval. The item list is derived from the draft on every lookup — the only stored state is the cursor and the dismissal.
- **`/login [provider]` / `/logout [provider]`** (credentials; §6.5) — a modal wizard rendered above the input rule, opened by the panel's full-width grey (`statusSep`) rule like every other panel (↑/↓ picks, `enter` commits, `esc`/`ctrl+c` backs out one level, then cancels — noted as `login cancelled`/`logout cancelled` in the scrollback). `/login` lists the configured providers with their methods (`... — API key`, `... — API key · subscription login`, `... — API key (subscription OAuth not permitted)`); an argument skips the picker. Providers with an OAuth flow get a method row (`API key` / `Subscription login`); everything else goes straight to key entry. The key is entered masked (`•••…`, capped at 40 dots with a char count beyond), is filtered of control bytes (paste included), is **never echoed or stored anywhere but auth.json**, and `enter` writes it via `store.PutAPIKey` — from the next request on the running session uses it (the credential is re-read per request). The OAuth row starts `provider.Login` in a command goroutine with the output bridged into the scrollback through the event pipe (so progress lines and the completion can never swap order); while it runs the panel says so, `enter` with text in the input box hands the pasted redirect URL/code to the flow (a blocked pipe write is spawned, released when the flow ends or is cancelled), and `esc` cancels the flow. After a successful OAuth login whose provider is not configured `auth: "oauth"`, the wizard asks `[y/N]` to flip `providers.<p>.auth` (`config.SetString`, comment-preserving) and reloads the config so the next `/clear` (or restart) picks the new mode up; `n`/`esc` leaves it unchanged. `/logout` revokes a subscription session best-effort (15 s bound, off the event loop) then clears the provider's token and API key; with no argument it picks from the providers that have a stored credential (`nothing stored — /login first` otherwise). Key material is never printed, and the login panel is sanitized like any other rendering.
- **`!cmd` / `!!cmd`**: user-initiated shell in the workdir (30s timeout, `ShellEnv` hygiene, output truncated 30K, `[exit N]`; `esc` kills a running `!` — `[interrupted — process group killed]`, and an interrupted command is not added to the transcript); `!` additionally appends `"$ cmd\n<output>\n[exit N]"` as a user message (bypasses the model-facing analyser, §7); `!!` is display-only. `!` is refused while a run is in progress, and a run/`/clear` is refused while a `!` is still executing (its note would land between a tool batch's results or in the wrong session; `!!` still runs).
- **Approvals** (v0.4.0-beta: a picker + the shortcuts): shell/MCP questions open with the panel's grey rule (v0.7.5) and render the question line `? allow \`x\`?` when the question can persist, `` ? allow `x` (never saved)? `` when only the session answer is available (no allow-always row), and `` ? allow `x` (asks every time)? `` when even that is excluded — the config file and a web redirect hop, §11 — followed by the picker rows — the options appear exactly once, as the picker; the question line used to repeat them as `[a] once  [ctrl+a] always  [d] deny` — `❯ Allow once o` · `Allow for this session s` (`Allow this exact command for this session` when the answer keys the command line — `SessionExact`: `rm`, a rule-Ask, auto's ask-every-time tier; v0.7.7) · `Allow always a` (only when `CanAlways`) · `Deny d · esc` — and then the full command/args under a dim `│` gutter — up to 6 rows (fewer on short terminals), each clamped to the width, the rest counted as `… +N lines · ctrl+o shows the whole command` (the subject alone can hide what runs: an approved `python3` executes the heredoc below its first line). The selection starts at **Allow once** (an "always" that persists must be chosen deliberately). `↑`/`↓` move the selection with wrap-around, and `enter` confirms it — both **only when the draft is empty and the 700 ms typing pause has elapsed** (the same `approvalIdle` guard the `o`/`s`/`a`/`d` shortcuts obey: a plain letter — a stray capital `A`, the first letter of "Add…" — and an enter meant to send a message always reach the draft instead — the pause, not the draft, is the guard, so the letters are safe mid-sentence but a deliberate `a` after the pause answers even over a draft (the caveat at the end of this bullet); with a non-empty draft the arrows keep their history/editing behaviour and enter submits). Confirming allow-always goes through the same `answerApproval` path as `a`, and the decision is **project-scoped** (rev 28): `shell` persists the subject as a `<workdir>/.moca/config.jsonc` `permissions.allow` rule and MCP persists the tool into that file's `mcp.servers.<name>.approve` (both via the comment-preserving `config.AppendString` — new files 0600/`MkdirAll` of `.moca`, existing files keep their mode; `.bak` + atomic rename; a symlinked config is edited through the link) — never into the user config, so one project's answers do not follow the user into every other directory. The running session learns the command with its answer (`Shell.Allow`/the MCP session allow) and the in-memory `/clear` restart copy learns both too, so nothing is lost in-session. A workdir that is not trusted cannot have project resources applied: the note then reads `always allowing "x" (saved to <path> — project rules apply once this workdir is trusted)` — the file is written, and the rule bites on the first trusted session there (nothing persists when there is no workdir at all: `(this session only)`). **Allow for this session** (v0.7.7, the `s` row) is the in-memory fourth answer: the same question is never asked again for the rest of the session, nothing is written, and it is offered on every question that can be remembered — `rm`, a rule-Ask and a prompts-root write included, where allow-always is not. The match tier is the question's (`SessionExact`): a name the rules could learn matches every invocation (`go` covers every `go …`, and the session's in-memory rules learn the name, so auto's classifier is skipped next time), and a name they cannot matches only the exact command line — `rm -f x` is not `rm -f y`; MCP keys `server/tool`, web `fetch <host>`, write the resolved path. The scope is one Agent: `/mode`, `/model` and `/effort` keep it, `/clear` and `/resume` start empty, and each ACP session has its own. The note reads `allowing \`x\` for this session` (the command line, not the name, on the exact tier — a multi-line command shows its first line and `…`, since the note is one scrollback line). One code path, so the picker's enter and the shortcuts cannot drift apart (pinned: `TestApprovalPickerArrowsEnter`, `TestApprovalPickerAlwaysPersists`, `TestApprovalShowsItsOptionsOnce`, `TestAllowAlwaysPersists`, `TestAllowAlwaysNotesAnUntrustedWorkdir`, `TestApprovalPickerNoAlwaysRow`, `TestApprovalPickerYieldsToDraft`, `TestApprovalPickerIdleGuard`, `TestApprovalSessionKey`, `TestApprovalSessionRowLabels`, `TestApprovalNoSessionRow`, `TestAllowAlwaysIsPlainA`, `TestApprovalKeysIgnoredWhileTyping`). `esc` always denies; plain `o`/`s`/`a`/`d` count only after the pause, a letter the question does not offer (`a` on an `rm`, `s` where it is excluded) is swallowed — handled, no answer, nothing typed — and `ctrl+a` no longer answers at all: it falls through to the input box's line start. `a` is the one key that **persists** (project-scoped, above), so a stray `a` after the pause writes the config even with a draft — the deliberate trade the v0.7.7 changelog leads with. `ctrl+c`/`enter` fall through to their normal meanings while a prompt with a draft is shown.
- **Bridge/threading**: the agent runs in a goroutine; `Emit` delivers events through a FIFO pipe (`eventPipe`) — the run's completion (`runDoneMsg`) rides the same pipe so it can never overtake a streamed delta and flush a half-streamed line — because `Program.Send` blocks on an unbuffered channel and `Update` runs on the event-loop goroutine (a synchronous emit — e.g. `/yolo` — would deadlock); the asker blocks the agent on a reply channel. The agent guards its shared state with a mutex, so the TUI's `Status()` polling during a run is race-free.
- **Rendering safety**: model/user/tool/error text goes through `Sanitize` (C0 controls → `^[`-style, DEL → `^?`, C1 controls U+0080–U+009F → their ESC form, e.g. U+009B → `^[[`) before it reaches the terminal — including the approval prompt and the status bar (sanitized *before* styling); styled lines are printed without sanitizing.

## 4. Config

- Path `~/.config/moca/config.jsonc` (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` honoured); data dir `~/.local/share/moca` (`$XDG_DATA_HOME`). Missing file → built-in defaults. Strict decode: **unknown keys are rejected**, and the file holds **exactly one JSON object** — trailing content (`{...} {...}`, `{...} junk`) is a config error rather than silently dropped, in the user config and in the project file alike (`TestParseRejectsTrailingContent`, `TestLoadProjectRejectsTrailingContent`); errors carry `line:col`.
- **JSONC pre-pass** (`config.Standardize`): comments (`//`, `/* */`) are recognised only outside string literals (escapes honoured) and replaced by spaces — output has the **same length** as the input, so error offsets still map to the original line:col. A trailing comma (`,` followed only by whitespace/comments before `}`/`]`) is blanked. Unterminated `/*` → error. The shipped config example is the byte-exact fixture (`internal/config/testdata/example.jsonc`).
- **The complete reference is `moca config sample` (v0.7.4)** — `internal/config/sample.jsonc`, printed byte for byte, with every key, its default and a comment per block; `moca config check` validates this file and the trusted project one and lists every issue (§3). `internal/config/testdata/example.jsonc` stays the byte-exact fixture of this section; the sample is the same schema made complete and is the file named to users.
- **Keys** (camelCase): `model`, `modelHard`, `providers.<name>` (`auth` = `api_key|oauth`, `apiKey` — optional; `"env:VAR"` when set — `baseUrl`, `baseUrls` per protocol, `protocol`, `models.<id>` with `protocol`/`contextWindow`/`maxOutputTokens`/`cost`), `shell.allow`, **`permissions`** (v0.4.0-beta, §11): `defaultMode` (`ask|auto|yolo|plan` — the legacy `default` accepted as `ask`; empty = derived — the legacy `yolo` flag, else **`auto`** since v0.7.2; `plan` = the plan scope, which rides on `ask`), `allow`/`ask`/`deny` (rule string arrays, §11), `auto.classifier` (bool, default **true**) and `auto.model` (the classifier's model; empty = `model`), `mcp.idleTimeout` + `mcp.servers.<name>` (command|url, args, env, headers, description, approve), `snapshot.retentionDays` (0 = forever), `context.reserveTokens|keepRecentTokens|maxSteps|progressNudgeAt` (the one-shot §14 progress nudge's tool-batch threshold; 0 = off, absent = 12), `providers.ollama` (local provider, §6), `web.search.provider` (`tavily|exa`) + `web.search.apiKey` (`env:` reference — tavily works without one, exa needs it), `tui.notify` (`osc9|bell|off`), `tui.theme` (user config only; v0.6.0: `both`/`dark`/`light` groups of color roles — per role a `#rrggbb` hex or an ANSI color number 0-255; §3.5), `yolo` (legacy flag; `yolo: true` conflicts with a `permissions.defaultMode` other than `yolo` — config error), **`skills.selfManaged`** (v0.7.0, §12 — tri-state in both layers; the user config's `true` is the only opt-in), **`skills.recordNudge`** (v0.7.1, §12.1 — a bool in both layers, **on by default**; a `false` anywhere turns the end-of-run recording nudge off and leaves the v0.7.0 mid-run hint, and no layer needs to set `true`).
- **Defaults**: built-in providers `anthropic`/`openai`/`opencode-go`/`opencode`/`openrouter` with `auth: api_key` and `env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `env:OPENAI_API_KEY` / `env:OPENCODE_API_KEY` (both OpenCode gateways share one account key; override per provider) / `env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY`; `shell.allow` = the 32-entry `DefaultShellAllow` list (`internal/config/config.go`); `permissions.defaultMode` empty (→ mode `default` unless legacy `yolo`), `permissions.auto.classifier` **on**; `tui.theme` empty (the built-in palette, `internal/tui/theme.go`); `mcp.idleTimeout` 600; `context` 16384/20000/40 with `progressNudgeAt` 12 (0 = off); `snapshot.retentionDays` 30; `web.search.provider` `tavily`; `tui.notify` `osc9`; `yolo` false; `skills.selfManaged` unset (off); `skills.recordNudge` unset (**on** — only a `false` decides anything, and it is inert while self-managed skills are off).
- **Permissions validation** (pinned: `TestPermissionsBlock`, `TestPermissionsValidation`): strict decode — unknown keys inside `permissions` are rejected like anywhere else (a `{"permissions":{"bogus":1}}` is a config error); `defaultMode` must be one of `ask|auto|yolo|plan` when set (the legacy `default` normalizes to `ask`); every `tui.theme` value must be a `#rrggbb` hex or an ANSI color number 0-255, and an unknown role is an unknown field (`TestThemeValidation`); every `allow/ask/deny` entry must be non-empty after whitespace splitting; `yolo: true` together with `permissions.defaultMode` ≠ `yolo`/unset is a config error. Rule syntax is fully parsed later by `agent.rules` — config validation only sanity-checks the words; an unparseable rule fails the session build loudly, never silently dropped.
- **The project file `.moca/config.jsonc`** (`config.LoadProject`, pinned: `TestLoadProject`, `TestLoadProjectMCP`, `TestLoadProjectIgnoredWhenUntrusted`): read **only when the workdir is trusted** (cmd decides; trusted = `--approve`/yolo/trust store) and only for `cfg.Project` — absent nowhere. May hold a `permissions` object, an `mcp` block and a `skills` object — **nothing else** (a cloned repository must not point providers, keys or the classifier model anywhere, or recolor the TUI; any other top-level key — a `tui` block included: colors and `notify` are user-only — malformed JSONC or rule syntax is a config error, exit 2, with the file's path and original `line:col`); may set `defaultMode` `default|auto|plan` (never `yolo` — a cloned repository cannot start permission-free sessions; use `--mode yolo`/`/yolo`) — it wins over the user's (§3; a folder contributes only after you trust it) — plus `allow`/`ask`/`deny` and `auto.classifier: false`; may **never** set `auto.model` (whose model bills the classifier is a user decision) or an allow rule whose first word is made only of `*` characters (`"*"`, `"**"`, `"* rm"` — "matches every command"). An untrusted workdir's project file is never read, so an error in it cannot block startup (`TestLoadProjectIgnoredWhenUntrusted`). In auto mode a project file's rules can only tighten: a rule match decides before the deterministic table (§11), and no classifier means unsures ask. Its **`skills` block** (§12, v0.7.0) carries `selfManaged`: `false` opts the repository out of self-managed skills, `true` is declarative — accepted, never an error, never enabling the feature on its own (it warns a user who has not opted in); it may also carry `recordNudge: false` (v0.7.1) to keep that repository free of the end-of-run nudge — a `true` is declarative and changes nothing, since the nudge is on by default. Its **`mcp` block** (§10.5) mirrors the user config's shape: `idleTimeout` (non-zero overrides the user's) and `servers.<name>` — either a full definition (`command` xor `url`, plus args/env/headers/description) or a patch of a server another layer defines (`approve` is the common case, and what moca itself writes); both transports at once, a `"*"` approve entry (a project may not blanket-approve a server it also controls) and an empty tool name are config errors. A project server starts only for a trusted workdir.
- **Self-managed skills, resolved (§4/§12, v0.7.0; `config.ResolveSelfManagedSkills`, pinned by `TestResolveSelfManagedSkillsTable`)** — the user config's `skills.selfManaged` is the only thing that can enable the feature; a trusted project file's `false` opts the repository out with the note `self-managed skills are off in this project: its .moca/config.jsonc sets skills.selfManaged false`, and a project `true` without a user opt-in is off with the warning `this project's .moca/config.jsonc sets skills.selfManaged true, but your user config does not enable it — self-managed skills are off; set "skills": {"selfManaged": true} in <user config> and restart moca to take part` (the `…sets it false…` wording when the user value is an explicit `false`). An untrusted workdir never reads the project file, so it gets no message. A switch change takes effect in a **new moca process** (the config is read once). **moca before v0.7.0 rejects any `skills` key in a project file** (strict decoding): a committed `skills.selfManaged` stops a trusted v0.6.x teammate from starting moca in that repository until they upgrade. **The recording nudge, resolved (§4/§12.1, v0.7.1; `config.ResolveRecordNudge`, pinned by `TestResolveRecordNudgeTable`)** — on unless the user config or the trusted project file sets `skills.recordNudge: false`; unlike `selfManaged` nothing has to enable it, a project `true` never overrides a user's `false`, and an untrusted workdir simply has no project layer to consult.
- **Validation**: model ids must be `provider/model` (split at first `/`); provider must exist; an `apiKey` literal → rejected (must be `env:`), an omitted `apiKey` → accepted (a store-only provider, resolved from `/login`); custom providers need `baseUrl`(+`baseUrls`) and `protocol`; model-level `protocol` must be one of the three; custom models must declare `contextWindow`; `contextWindow < 16384` refused; context values and `retentionDays >= 0` checked; each MCP server must have exactly one of `command`/`url`; `mcp.idleTimeout` must not be negative (0 = the default 600); `tui.notify` must be `osc9`, `bell` or `off`; `auth: "oauth"` is rejected for a provider the §3 policy gate refuses (anthropic — the error carries the recorded reason, §6.5) and for any provider without an implemented OAuth flow (`config.OAuthProviders`, pinned against `provider.oauthProviders` by a test — "no OAuth support for this provider; set \"api_key\"").
- **Web search validation**: `web.search.provider` must be `tavily` or `exa`; a literal `web.search.apiKey` → rejected (must be an `env:` reference); `exa` without one → rejected (exa has no keyless mode); an `apiKey` whose `env:` variable is unset → config error at session start (exit 2 — the key is resolved eagerly, before the first request, unlike provider keys); a configured key joins the shell-env strip (`EnvRefs`).
- **Log**: `log.level` — `info` (default) | `debug` | `off`; anything else → config error. The diagnostic log (§13.5); `MOCA_LOG` overrides it for one run.
- **Env indirection**: `env:VAR` resolved lazily — an unset variable errors **when the first request to that provider is built** (naming the variable, `*config.EnvError` → exit 2), never at startup; unused providers don't block. For `api_key` providers resolution is **store-first**: a key stored in auth.json (§6.5, `/login` or `moca login`) wins over the config's `env:VAR` reference; the error when neither exists names the variable and both login entry points. `config.EnvRefs` lists every referenced variable (used by phase 2's shell hygiene).
- **Custom providers** (vLLM/LM Studio/Ollama…): any name + `baseUrl` + `protocol` + models with `contextWindow`; usable through the same registry path.
- **`config.AppendString(path, keyPath, value, init)`** (phase 3) — comment-preserving JSONC edit: appends `value` to the string array at `keyPath` (creating missing objects / the file, 0600), no-op when present, everything else byte-identical (string-literal-aware `Standardize` + an offset-tracking scanner; trailing commas and `//`/`/* */` comments survive, also those containing commas). Writes `<path>.bak` then an atomic rename. Used by the TUI's allow-always and by MCP approvals — since rev 28 both target the workdir's `.moca/config.jsonc` (`permissions.allow` / `mcp.servers.<name>.approve`), never the user config.
- **`config.SetObjectEntry(path, keyPath, key, rawJSON)`** (phase 5) — inserts `"key": rawJSON` into the object at `keyPath` with the same guarantees as `AppendString` (missing parents/file, `.bak` + atomic rename, mode/symlink preservation, byte-identical elsewhere); an existing key is left untouched (`added=false`); a non-JSON value is refused before any write. Used by `moca mcp import`.
- **`config.SetString(path, keyPath, key, value)`** (phase 7) — replaces an existing string value in place (the same standardized-text offset scanner; `.bak` + atomic rename, mode/symlink preservation; falls back to `SetObjectEntry` when the key or path is missing). Duplicate keys resolve to the last occurrence — the value `encoding/json` keeps, so the edit lands where it takes effect; a non-string prior value is refused, never replaced. Used by `moca login`'s offer to flip `providers.<name>.auth` to `"oauth"`.

- **Model-list cache** (`<data>/models-cache/`, v0.7.10): the model lists discovered from the providers themselves (§6), **one file per provider** — `<provider>.json`, the name kept to `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` with every other byte written `%XX`, so any config key is a portable file name inside the directory. Each file is a JSON object: `version` 1, `provider` (a file whose recorded provider differs is a miss — `GW` and `gw` share a file on a case-insensitive file system), `baseUrl`, `fetchedAt` and the normalized models; 24 h TTL, atomic replace, mode 0600, metadata only (never a key, a prompt or a response). A provider's discovery reads and rewrites only its own file, so parallel discovery and two moca processes need no lock. A corrupt or older-version file reads as empty. `/clear-models-cache` (TUI) deletes every file and re-reads the current model's provider at once (§6).

## 5. Model catalog, effort, thinking

`internal/provider/catalog.go` — `Model{Provider, ID, Protocol, ContextWindow, MaxOutput, Cost(USD/M input/output/cacheRead/cacheWrite), ThinkingMode, ThinkingLevelMap, CostKnown, WindowAssumed}`.

**Catalog rows are seed metadata, not the model list** (v0.7.10): a provider's models are discovered from the provider itself (§6) and merged *under* these rows — curated protocol/limits/cost/thinking always win, and a discovered id the catalog does not describe is added from the provider's own payload: with `CostKnown:false` when the payload carries no price (the UI renders `—`, never `$0.00`) and `WindowAssumed:true` when it carries no window (16384 is substituted and the startup warning says so). OpenRouter publishes both, so its rows arrive priced and windowed. A window or cost declared under `providers.<name>.models.<id>` clears both flags.

**Built-in catalog** (prices/limits verified 2026-10-04, opencode-go expansion rows 2026-10-07 — re-verify against the sources listed in the file's comment when vendor pages change; cost = standard rates; the deepseek rows use the off-peak rate):

| provider | model | protocol | ctx | max out | in/out/cacheR/cacheW $/M | thinking |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| opencode-go | glm-5.3 | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 131,072 | 1.4 / 4.4 / 0.26 / 0 | openai + glmThinking |
| opencode-go | glm-5.3-flash | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 131,072 | 0.15 / 0.5 / 0.03 / 0 | openai + glmFlashThinking |
| opencode-go | minimax-m3 | anthropic-messages | 1,000,000 | 131,072 | 0.3 / 1.2 / 0.06 / 0 | budget |
| opencode-go | kimi-k3 | openai-completions | 1,048,576 | 131,072 | 3 / 15 / 0.3 / 0 | openai + kimiThinking |
| opencode-go | gpt-6-luna | openai-responses | 1,050,000 | 128,000 | 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.01 / 0.125 | openai + openaiReasoning |
| opencode-go | grok-4.7 | openai-responses | 500,000 | 500,000 | 2 / 6 / 0.5 / 0 | openai + openaiReasoning |
| opencode-go | deepseek-v4.1-flash | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 384,000 | 0.15 / 0.6 / 0.003 / 0 | openai + deepseekThinking |
| opencode-go | deepseek-v4-flash | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 384,000 | 0.15 / 0.6 / 0.003 / 0 | openai + deepseekThinking |
| opencode-go | deepseek-v4-pro | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 384,000 | 0.66 / 1.98 / 0.022 / 0 | openai + deepseekProThinking |
| opencode-go | deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 384,000 | 0.15 / 0.6 / 0.003 / 0 | openai + deepseekThinking |
| opencode-go | kimi-k2.7-code | openai-completions | 262,144 | 262,144 | 0.95 / 4 / 0.19 / 0 | openai + basicThinking |
| opencode-go | mimo-v2.6-pro | openai-completions | 1,048,576 | 131,072 | 0.435 / 0.87 / 0.003625 / 0 | openai + basicThinking |
| opencode-go | mimo-v2.6-flash | openai-completions | 1,048,576 | 131,072 | 0.14 / 0.28 / 0.0028 / 0 | openai + basicThinking |
| opencode-go | qwen3.8-max | openai-completions | 1,000,000 | 131,072 | 2 / 6 / 0.25 / 2.5 | openai + qwenThinking |
| opencode-go | qwen3.8-flash | anthropic-messages | 1,000,000 | 131,072 | 0.15 / 0.47 / 0.016 / 0.2 | budget |
| opencode-go | qwen3.7-plus | anthropic-messages | 1,000,000 | 65,536 | 0.4 / 1.6 / 0.04 / 0.5 | budget |
| anthropic | claude-opus-5-5 | anthropic-messages | 1,000,000 | 128,000 | 4 / 20 / 0.2 / 5 | adaptive |
| anthropic | claude-sonnet-5-5 | anthropic-messages | 1,000,000 | 128,000 | 2 / 10 / 0.2 / 2.5 | adaptive |
| anthropic | claude-haiku-4-5 | anthropic-messages | 200,000 | 64,000 | 1 / 5 / 0.1 / 1.25 | budget |
| openai | gpt-6-astra | openai-responses | 1,050,000 | 128,000 | 10 / 50 / 1 / 12.5 | openai + openaiReasoning |
| openai | gpt-6-luna | openai-responses | 1,050,000 | 128,000 | 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.01 / 0.125 | openai + openaiReasoning |

**Thinking modes & level maps** (a map value is the wire value; a missing key = unsupported level):

- `none` — no thinking; every effort clamps to `off`.
- `budget` (anthropic manual mode) — sends `thinking: {type:"enabled", budget_tokens:N}`; omitted when effort is `off` **or** `N == 0`.
- `adaptive` (current Claude) — sends `thinking: {type:"adaptive", display:"summarized"}` + `output_config.effort`; map `anthropicAdaptive` = {low, medium, high, xhigh, max} (so `off`/`minimal` clamp up to `low`; `xhigh` is sent as-is since v0.6.2 — it used to clamp down to `high`).
- `openai` — completions: `reasoning_effort`; responses: `reasoning: {effort, summary:"auto"}`. Maps: `openaiReasoning` = {minimal, low, medium, high}; `glmThinking` = {minimal, low, medium, high, max}; `glmFlashThinking` = {low, high, max}; `kimiThinking` = {off:"none", minimal, low, medium, high, max}; `deepseekThinking` = {low, high, max}; `deepseekProThinking` = {high, max}; `qwenThinking` = {low, medium, xhigh}; `basicThinking` = {low, medium, high} (models whose gateway rejects `off`/`minimal` — kimi-k2.7-code, mimo-v2.6-*). The two glm maps have **no `off`**: the serving variants became thinking-only (live-verified 2026-10-16, §6), so `off` clamps to the lowest supported level and the model list's thinking column must name the map — `glm-5.3` → `glmThinking`, `glm-5.3-flash` → `glmFlashThinking`, `kimi-k3` → `kimiThinking`.

**Effort clamping** (`Model.ClampEffort`): nearest supported level **at or below** the request; if none is below, the lowest supported; `off` on a model that cannot disable → lowest supported.

**Budget** (`Model.BudgetTokens(maxTokens, effort)`): `maxTokens < 2048` → `0` (thinking omitted — the API floor is 1024 and 1024 must stay free for the answer); else a share of `maxTokens`: minimal/low 25 %, medium 50 %, high 75 %, max 100 %, floored at 1024 and capped at `maxTokens − 1024`. (Effort is pre-clamped by the adapter, so only mapped levels arrive; `off` never sends thinking.)

**Max output** (`Model.MaxTokens(reserve)`): `min(MaxOutput, reserve)` — reserve = `context.reserveTokens` (16384).

**Cost** (`Model.CostOf(usage)`): `Σ tokens × rate / 1e6`.

## 6. Providers & registry

`provider.NewRegistry(cfg, httpClient, notify)` builds the catalog (built-ins + seeds + config overrides/additions) and makes **no network call**. Since v0.7.10 it does not reject a `model`/`modelHard` the catalog does not know — the id may exist upstream — so `Registry.DiscoverModels` (run by `agent.Start`/`Resume`, by ACP's model check and by a stale `/model`) fills in each provider's list and `Registry.Verify()` becomes the gate, naming the nearest discovered ids when it fails. A nil `httpClient` means the shared header-bounded default (§7), never a nil client: discovery, the adapters and an OAuth refresh all go through it.

- **Default base URLs** — `anthropic` → `https://api.anthropic.com`; `openai` → `https://api.openai.com/v1`; `opencode-go` → anthropic-messages `https://opencode.ai/zen/go`, openai-* `https://opencode.ai/zen/go/v1`; `opencode` (the Zen pay-as-you-go tier) → openai-completions only, `https://opencode.ai/zen/v1`; `openrouter` → `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1`; `ollama` → the local rules below. **Precedence**: `baseUrls[protocol]` > `baseUrl` > built-in default.
- **Model discovery (v0.7.10)** — every registered provider publishes its model list; `provider.DiscoverModels` fetches them, normalizes into `CachedModel` and merges. Endpoints (verified live 2026-10-24): `anthropic` → `GET {base}/v1/models?limit=1000`, paginated via `after_id`/`has_more`, carrying `max_input_tokens`, `max_tokens`, `capabilities.effort.*` and `capabilities.thinking.types.*` (so a discovered Claude row gets its real window, output cap, thinking mode and effort set); `openai` → `GET {base}/models` (id + `shutdown_date`; non-chat ids filtered by a documented substring deny-list: embedding/tts/whisper/dall-e/moderation/image/audio/realtime/transcribe); `opencode-go`, `opencode`, `openrouter` and custom providers → `GET {base}/models`; `openrouter` additionally carries `context_length`, `top_provider.max_completion_tokens`, per-token `pricing.*` and `reasoning.supported_efforts`/`mandatory` (image/audio/:free/:batch/router ids filtered). Ollama keeps its native tags+show discovery (§6).

  **Rules**: discovery runs per session start and on a stale `/model`, in parallel, with a 4 s cap per provider, and is **fail-open** — a provider that errors keeps its cached or catalog rows and never fails a run. A provider is fetched only when its credential resolves (a stored key, a resolvable `env:` reference, a configured local provider), so an unauthenticated provider is neither fetched nor listed. A **custom provider with declared models is not discovered**: for an arbitrary gateway the declaration is the contract. Precedence on merge is `config override > catalog row > discovered row > defaults`. The 24 h cache (§4) is consulted first: fresh entries make no network call; stale entries are served while the refetch runs. `/clear-models-cache` deletes every provider's file and re-reads the current model's provider at once (Ollama, which has no file, included), off the UI loop with a 6 s cap; no row leaves the registry — the other providers' rows are replaced by their next discovery, which the deleted files can no longer serve, and a failed re-read leaves the provider's rows as they were. **A resolved model stays resolvable**: every row `Resolve` hands out or `Verify` checks (`model`, `modelHard`, `permissions.auto.model`) is remembered for the registry's life, so the summarizer, the auto-mode judge and `/hard` — which re-resolve by name — survive a relist that drops their id; the live row wins when present, and a `/model` switch (`CheckCredential`) still sees the live catalog only. `Registry.ActiveModels()` is the picker's list (models whose provider can run); ACP's `model` option uses the same filter.

**OpenRouter (`openrouter`, v0.7.10)** — a built-in provider over `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` (`openai-completions`), key from `env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY` (store-first like every `api_key` provider) or `/login openrouter`. Model ids contain slashes, so a reference is three segments (`openrouter/anthropic/claude-sonnet-5.5`) — safe because `SplitModel` cuts at the first `/` and every consumer treats a qualified id as opaque. Requests carry `X-OpenRouter-Title: moca`, `HTTP-Referer: https://github.com/adeotek/moca` and `x-session-id: <req.CacheKey>` (sticky routing keeps its prompt cache warm). Thinking uses the `reasoning:{effort}` object mode (`effort-object`), which is the only documented shape carrying `max`.

**OpenCode Zen (`opencode`, v0.7.10)** — the pay-as-you-go gateway at `https://opencode.ai/zen/v1` (`openai-completions`), on the same account key as the go tier by default (`env:OPENCODE_API_KEY`, overridable per provider). It needs **no** `x-opencode-session` header (that is the go tier's). Its catalog rows are borrowed from `opencode-go` by model id (`catalogSeeds`): same vendor, same published per-token prices — so a discovered zen id the go catalog describes keeps its window, cost and thinking map with `Provider: "opencode"`.

**Model overrides merge, not replace**: a config entry for a catalog model changes only the fields it sets; a new model under a custom provider needs `protocol` + `contextWindow` (config validation); a new model under a **built-in** provider needs `protocol` (model-level or provider-level) — otherwise `NewRegistry` fails fast with the exact key path to add.
- **Credentials resolve store-first** (§6.5): an `api_key` provider reads the credential store (auth.json, 0600) at request time — a key stored with `/login` (TUI) or `moca login <provider>` wins over the config's optional `env:VAR` reference, which is the fallback; neither → a clear error naming the variable and both login entry points (`*config.EnvError` → exit 2). Because the store is read per request, a key stored mid-session applies to the next request. `oauth` resolves through the store's token entry — the registry hands the adapter a store-backed credential that refreshes on demand, and a missing or revoked login errors with "run `/login` in the TUI, or moca login" (exit 2). Registry rule: an `oauth` provider resolves only for models whose protocol is `openai-responses` (the error names the protocol).
- **OpenCode Go routing header**: every `opencode-go` request carries a stable per-conversation `x-opencode-session` — the session's `req.CacheKey` when the request carries one (so a TUI's `/resume` or an ACP server's several sessions each keep their own cache shard), else the process-wide `crypto/rand` id generated at registry construction (`TestOpencodeSessionHeaderFollowsCacheKey`) and every request from every provider identifies the client as `User-Agent: moca/<version>`. The Go tier rejects session-less requests with HTTP 400 `MissingSessionID` (verified live 2026-10-05, https://opencode.ai/docs/go/#where-can-i-use-it); zen pay-as-you-go (`/zen/v1`) does not require it.
- **Thinking/effort maps** (`ThinkingLevelMap`) — the map definitions, `ClampEffort` and the per-model rows live in §5. **glm maps re-verified live 2026-10-16** against the zen go tier: `glm-5.3-flash` became thinking-only — it accepts only `low|high|max` (`none` → `[1210] … cannot be disabled; please use low, high, or max`), so moca's default `medium` clamps to `low` before the wire (it used to be sent as-is and 400'd mid-run); `glm-5.3` accepts `minimal|low|medium|high|max`; `kimi-k3` still disables thinking through `none`. Pinned by `TestGLMEffortMapsMatchTheThinkingOnlyServingVariants`.
- **Ollama (`ollama`, a local-provider)** — a local or LAN model server over its OpenAI-compatible endpoint (`openai-completions`, the existing adapter). `config.LocalProviders` = `[ollama]`: **opt-in** — it exists only when `providers.ollama` is present (an empty `{}` is enough: no `baseUrl`, `protocol`, key or model list needed; `auth` defaults to `api_key`) or when `model`/`modelHard`/`--model` names an `ollama/…` model (`Config.UseProvider`, which copies the Providers map). Nothing is probed for anyone who did not opt in.
  - **Base URL**: `providers.ollama.baseUrl` > `$OLLAMA_HOST` (the variable Ollama's own CLI honors) > `http://localhost:11434`. Any form people write is normalized (`nas` · `nas:8080` · `:11434` · `http://nas:11434` · `https://host/prefix/v1`): scheme `http` and port 11434 default in, `0.0.0.0` means `127.0.0.1`, and `/v1` is appended to a bare host (an explicit path is kept). LAN use: the *server* must listen beyond loopback (`OLLAMA_HOST=0.0.0.0`).
  - **No credential needed**: with no stored key and no `apiKey`, requests carry no `Authorization` header; a key stored with `/login ollama` (or an `env:` `apiKey`) is sent as a bearer token — for a server behind an authenticating proxy. A configured-but-unset `apiKey` env var is still an error.
  - **Model discovery** (`Registry.DiscoverOllama`, ≤ 4 s, best effort, run by `agent.Start`/`Resume` after `NewRegistry` and again by `/model`): `GET /api/tags`, then `POST /api/show` per model (8 in parallel). Embedding models and models without the `tools` capability are not offered (a failed `show`, or a server too old to report capabilities, keeps the model); `thinking` capability → `ThinkingMode openai` with levels `off`→`none`, `low`/`medium`/`high` (`reasoning_effort`); `MaxOutput` = window/4 clamped to 2048–16384; cost 0. A rediscovery replaces the previous ollama models (pulled ones appear, removed ones go). **Models declared under `providers.ollama.models.<id>` always exist and override what was discovered** (`contextWindow`, `maxOutputTokens`, `cost`); a declared-only model defaults to `openai-completions` and the assumed window.
  - **Context window**: the OpenAI-compatible endpoint cannot set `num_ctx` per request, so the real window is whatever the server runs with. moca takes `num_ctx` from the model's Modelfile parameters (`/api/show`); otherwise it **assumes 16384** (`config.MinContextWindow`). `agent.Start` emits a `Warning` when the *selected* model's window was assumed (set `OLLAMA_CONTEXT_LENGTH` on the server or declare `contextWindow` to match — a smaller real window silently truncates the prompt, and usage-anchored compaction never notices) or is below 16384 (too small for the system prompt and tools).
  - **Startup**: `NewRegistry` makes no network call and does not reject an `ollama/…` default/hard model; `Registry.Verify()` (after discovery) does, citing why — the server is unreachable, or `has no model "x" with tool support — pull it (ollama pull x) or declare it`. An unreachable server is *not* an error when the default model lives elsewhere.
  - **Failures**: a request that cannot reach the server (refused, no route, DNS, dial timeout) fails at once with `cannot reach ollama at <url> (<cause>) — is “ollama serve” running? …` — not retried (`ollamaGuard`, outside the retry wrapper; mid-stream resets still retry); HTTP 404 `model … not found` appends `pull it first: ollama pull <model>`; a `does not support tools` 400 suggests a tool-capable model; discovery answering 401/403 points to `/login ollama`.
- `Resolve("provider/model")` → `(Model, Adapter)`; the adapter is already wrapped in the retry policy. `Models()` returns the catalog sorted (for phase 3's `/model`).

## 6.5 Subscription OAuth (phase 7)

**Policy gate (binding).** Before any OAuth code for a provider ships, the vendor's current terms must permit third-party clients to use subscription credentials, and the registration path for a third-party client must be known; `unclear` counts as not permitted — no workarounds, no spoofed client identities. Record: `docs/specs/oauth-verification.md` (all URLs fetched live 2026-10-06). Outcomes: **anthropic — `api_key` only** (Anthropic does not permit third-party clients to use Claude subscription OAuth; `providers.anthropic.auth: "oauth"` is a config error naming the record); **openai — OAuth ships** via Sign in with ChatGPT (SIWC) open-source token sharing, and only on the `openai-responses` protocol.

- **Credential store** (`internal/provider/auth.go`) — `~/.config/moca/auth.json` (file 0600, dir 0700; the write forces 0600 even when a stale tmp file exists, and the rename deliberately replaces a symlink rather than following it). Two sections: `providers.<name>` holds the OAuth token — access/refresh tokens, expiry, account id, client id, id token, email, granted scopes — and `apiKeys.<name>` holds a provider API key stored with `/login` (TUI) or `moca login <provider>` (validated: empty values and control bytes are refused, so a credential can never smuggle a newline into a header). Atomic writes (temp file + rename). Every read-modify-write runs under a cross-process exclusive lock (`flock` on Unix, `LockFileEx` on Windows), so concurrent moca processes cannot clobber each other's rotated tokens; reads (including a per-request API-key lookup) are lock-free. **Migration**: a v0.1 store in the data dir is moved once at startup (`provider.MigrateLegacyStore`, called from `main`) — content and mode preserved, the old file only removed after the new one is in place, best-effort with at most one warning.
- **Login** (`moca login openai`, or `/login` in the TUI) — dynamic client registration (no client secret; the issued client_id is stored with the token), PKCE S256, state + nonce, loopback callback on `127.0.0.1` (the redirect host must be the loopback literal). The CLI opens the browser unless `--no-browser`; headless fallback prints the URL and reads the pasted code from stdin (one shared line reader serves the paste prompt and the later "switch auth?" prompt, so an unanswered paste read never swallows that answer; the loopback page reports a refused login with a 400, not "Login complete") (a pasted redirect URL that lost its scheme is recovered, and the state is verified in every form that carries one). The TUI wizard runs the same flow in a command goroutine with its output bridged into the scrollback, accepts the pasted redirect URL/code from the input box while it waits, and cancels on `esc`. Each login HTTP call (token exchange, JWKS) is bounded by a 30 s client timeout. The ID token is validated (RS256 against the provider JWKS with kid selection, issuer, audience, nonce) and the required scope (`chatgpt.tokens.use.direct`) must be present. An entropy failure aborts the login (state/nonce are never empty); a callback/paste race resolves first-wins on one shared channel. Refusals (`ErrInvalidGrant`/`ErrInvalidClient`) are usage errors → exit 2 with a login hint. Providers without an OAuth flow take an API key instead — `moca login <provider>` (prompt only on a terminal; piped stdin stores silently) or the TUI wizard's masked entry — written to `apiKeys.<provider>`.
- **Refresh** — access tokens ~1 h, refresh tokens rotate (~30 d). A refresh holds the store lock across read → refresh → write, so a concurrent process re-reads the fresh token instead of burning a rotated one; the lock wait honours the caller's context (polling, so Ctrl-C works) and the exchange is bounded at 30 s. `invalid_grant` keeps the stored entry (so `moca logout` still works) and errors "session expired … run `/login` in the TUI, or `moca login`" (exit 2). A transient failure (network, 429/5xx, timeout) is an `*HTTPError`/network error the retry layer retries, and while the access token still has time left it is used as is. `Registry.CheckCredential` (the `/model` and resume check) only confirms a login is stored — it never refreshes.
- **Wire use** — the subscription credential is a bearer on the Responses API with `store:false`; on this route moca omits `max_output_tokens` and sends the eight tools grouped in a single `{type:"namespace", name:"moca"}` group (the SIWC tool-shaping contract); the stream handling is unchanged.
- **`moca logout openai` / `/logout`** — best-effort revoke at the provider (bounded at 15 s — a stalled endpoint cannot hang the command), then deletes the provider's entry — OAuth token **and** stored API key. The TUI variant runs the revoke off the event loop and its argument-less form picks from the providers that have a stored credential.
- **Known limitation**: the client_id is registered per login (no shared app client id yet); no Anthropic OAuth by policy.

## 7. Protocol adapters (wire behavior)

All three: `post()` sends JSON with `Content-Type: application/json`, `Accept: text/event-stream`, `User-Agent: moca/<version>`; **a 2xx whose Content-Type is set and not `text/event-stream` is an error** ("unexpected … response, want text/event-stream: <body≤8KiB>", non-retryable) instead of a fake turn. Response bodies are closed on every path; stall timeout 90 s. Requests use a client with a 120 s response-header bound (`provider.defaultHTTPClient`, built once per process, clones `http.DefaultTransport` and sets `ResponseHeaderTimeout`; `NewRegistry` uses it whenever its caller passes nil — `agent.Start`, `moca acp`'s model check; tests may inject their own) — the stall bound only covers the response body, so without it a provider that accepts a request and never answers could hang an unattended run (observed once in the live ship-gate runs).

**anthropic-messages** — `POST {base}/v1/messages`; headers `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, `x-api-key` (or `Authorization: Bearer` for OAuth tokens).
- Body: `system` as one text block; `tools` (last tool carries `cache_control`); `tool_choice`; messages; thinking per §5. Cache breakpoints: system end + last tool + **the last eligible block of the last message** (text non-empty / tool_use / tool_result — never thinking/redacted/empty-text; walks back; omitted if none). `NoCacheWrite` (compaction) removes every `cache_control`.
- Stream: `message_start` (usage in), `content_block_*` (text/thinking/redacted_thinking/tool_use; `input_json_delta` assembled per index), `message_delta` (stop reason + output usage), `message_stop`. Missing `message_stop` at clean EOF → `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF` (retryable). In-stream `overloaded_error` → `*HTTPError{529}`. HTTP 400 "prompt is too long" → `ErrContextOverflow`.
- Thinking capture: `Model` = request model, `Signature` from `signature_delta`; `redacted_thinking` → `Redacted=true`, opaque `data` in `Signature`, text `[Reasoning redacted]`.
- Stop mapping: `tool_use`→tool_use, `max_tokens`/`model_context_window_exceeded`→length, `refusal`→refusal, else end_turn.

**openai-completions** — `POST {base}/chat/completions`; `Authorization: Bearer`. Body: system first; per message, text joined, tool calls with `arguments` as a JSON string, tool results as `role:"tool"`; `stream:true`, `stream_options.include_usage`; `reasoning_effort` per §5. A thinking block from history goes back as the assistant message's `reasoning_content` — **only** on a route whose catalog row sets `ReplayReasoning` (the four DeepSeek rows), because DeepSeek's thinking mode answers `400 … The reasoning_content in the thinking mode must be passed back to the API` when a replayed tool-call turn drops it; only the model's own blocks are echoed (compared by **qualified** id — the stamp the agent persists, §14), a redacted or empty block is dropped, and every other route sends none (no wire slot). Stream chunks: `content`, `reasoning_content`/`reasoning` (→ thinking events), `tool_calls[]` assembled **per `index`** (id/name/args may be split across deltas), `finish_reason`, usage (`prompt_tokens − cached_tokens` → Input, `cached_tokens` → CacheRead). Terminal: `[DONE]` or any `finish_reason`; otherwise `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF`. `content_filter`→refusal, `length`→length, `tool_calls`→tool_use; calls present with a `stop`/missing reason → tool_use. Overflow: 400 with `context_length_exceeded` / `maximum context length`.

**openai-responses** — `POST {base}/responses`; `Authorization: Bearer`. Body: `input` items (message text in/out, reasoning items with `encrypted_content`, function_call/function_call_output), `store:false`, `include:["reasoning.encrypted_content"]` (stateless reasoning replay), `instructions` = system, tools as `{type:function,…}`, `reasoning` per §5. Stream: `response.output_text.delta`, `response.reasoning_summary_text.delta` (accumulated per `item_id` as a fallback for the final item's summary), `response.output_item.done` (reasoning → thinking block with `ThinkingID`+`Signature`; function_call → call), `response.completed|incomplete` (usage; `incomplete`+`max_output_tokens`→length), `response.failed` (context_length_exceeded → `ErrContextOverflow`; server_error/rate_limit_exceeded → retryable 500). Terminal: completed/incomplete; otherwise `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF`. On the subscription (OAuth) route the body differs (§6.5): `max_output_tokens` is omitted and the tools go in one `{type:"namespace", name:"moca"}` group; the stream is unchanged.

**Tool calls — kept, never dropped.** Arguments are assembled and kept **as received**, even when cut off mid-JSON (`{}` only when nothing arrived). This is the phase-2 contract: the agent loop attaches a §6 error result ("your output was cut off at the token limit… split the work") to **every** call of a `length`-stopped turn, and the tool registry rejects invalid JSON with its own error result. A `tool_use` block that never received `content_block_stop` is kept with a nil input (not emitted). Calls are emitted to the event stream in order (anthropic: at `content_block_stop`; the OpenAI protocols: after the stream ends, index order). The anthropic assembler treats a **structurally corrupt stream** as an error, never a panic (F-3): a negative content-block index, or an `input_json_delta` addressed to a block that is not a `tool_use` (it used to dereference a nil builder) — `TestAnthropicCorruptStreamIsAnError`. The stop reason is **never** force-upgraded from `length`/`refusal`; `end`+calls → `tool_use`.

**History replay** (`replay.go`):
- Thinking replays **verbatim** (anthropic `thinking`+signature / `redacted_thinking`+data; responses reasoning item) only when `block.Model == request model` **and** `Signature != ""` (responses also needs `ThinkingID` and not redacted). Anything else degrades to a text block `[prior reasoning]\n<text>` (anthropic / responses-as-output_text); redacted or empty-text blocks are dropped. Rationale: signatures/encrypted payloads are bound to the producing model — replaying them elsewhere (or unsigned) is a 400 on every later turn.
- Tool input on replay: nil or invalid JSON → `{}` in all three adapters (invalid `json.RawMessage` would otherwise fail `json.Marshal` or send `"arguments": ""`).

**Usage normalization** (all protocols): `Input` excludes cache reads/writes; `CacheRead`/`CacheWrite` carried separately; `Usage.Add` accumulates.

## 8. Retry, errors, exit mapping

`WithRetry(adapter, DefaultRetryPolicy(notify))` — uniform across protocols.

- **Retryable**: HTTP 408/429/5xx (incl. anthropic 529) and HTTP 400 whose body is led by `"type":"server_error"` (the opencode-go gateway frames some upstream failures that way — the `[1210]` thinking-config flake; the server calls it a server error, so it is retried, bounded by the five-attempt budget); `ErrStall`; `io.EOF`, `io.ErrUnexpectedEOF`; `ECONNRESET`, `ECONNREFUSED`, `ECONNABORTED`, `EPIPE`; net timeouts; HTTP/2 GOAWAY / stream-reset (matched by error text — the stdlib types are unexported). **Not retryable**: other 4xx, `ErrContextOverflow`, `context.Canceled`, non-SSE 2xx.
- **Schedule**: 1/2/4/8/16 s (±20 % jitter), max 5 attempts, only while nothing has been emitted. `Retry-After` (integer, fractional or HTTP-date; ≤0 → 0) overrides the wait when larger; the wait is capped at 60 s.
- **Mid-stream failure** (anything already emitted): emit `EventReset` (consumers discard partial output), pause (jittered first delay, retry-after honoured — announced via `Notify` like any retry), retry the same request **once**; a second failure surfaces. This retry is outside the 5-attempt budget.
- **Stall**: no bytes for 90 s → `ErrStall` (aborts the attempt; retried).
- **Errors**: `*HTTPError{Status, RetryAfter, Body≤8KiB}`; `ErrStall`; `ErrContextOverflow` (wrapped by adapters); `*config.EnvError`; `provider.ErrInvalidGrant`/`ErrInvalidClient` (OAuth — user-fixable). `cmd` maps: `EnvError`→2, `ErrInvalidGrant`/`ErrInvalidClient`→2, cancellation/ctx→130, everything else→1.

## 9. `-p` run semantics

`-p` builds the full agent (§14): resolves the model, clamps the effort, and runs one conversation whose workdir is the process cwd. Trust for the run: explicit `--approve`/`--no-approve` wins, else yolo → trusted, else **no** (project `.moca/**` and `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` are ignored). Session slug = first five words of the prompt. `--resume`/`--continue` replace the fresh start with `agent.Resume` (stored prompt/workdir/model; trust and slug apply to fresh sessions only). The run ends when the model answers without tool calls; `context.maxSteps` batches trigger one wrap-up request (`tool_choice: none`, tools still listed) and exit 3.

## 9.5 ACP server (`moca acp`)

`moca acp` serves the Agent Client Protocol (v1, `protocolVersion: 1`) on stdin/stdout so ACP clients (Zed, VS Code ACP extensions, Paseo) drive moca. Editor config is a command line: `"agent_servers": {"moca": {"command": "moca", "args": ["acp"]}}`. Package `internal/acp` (stdlib only, no SDK, mirrors `internal/mcp`): `rpc.go` (bidirectional ndjson JSON-RPC 2.0 `Conn`), `protocol.go` (the ACP subset), `server.go`, `session.go`, plus the tool-call, replay, command and mode/config mappings. One ACP session = one `*agent.Agent`; the ACP `sessionId` **is** moca's session id8, so `session.Find`/`session.ListAll` resolve it. The TUI and `-p` paths are untouched.

**CLI and process.** `moca acp [--model <provider/model>] [--effort <level>] [--mode default|auto|yolo|plan] [--approve | --no-approve]` (its own `flag.FlagSet` over `o.Sub[1:]`; global flags before `acp` also apply). A bad flag, mode or effort, or `--approve` with `--no-approve`, exits 2 with usage on **stderr**. Config is loaded once at startup (a parse failure exits 2); a missing `model` is not fatal — `session/new` reports it. The file log starts as for the other modes (§13.5, `mode=acp`; metadata only: the method, the session id8, trust decisions, session open/close/delete and prompt-failure lines — never prompt text, file contents, command text or keys). **stdout carries JSON-RPC only**; nothing but `Conn` writes to it, and **stderr is silent** — the editor owns that pipe (Zed on Windows has been reported to lose, even kill, a child over a write to it), so a diagnostic log that cannot be opened drops its usual warning here and a serve error is logged instead of printed (`cmd/moca`). **A handler panic answers, it does not crash**: `Conn` recovers on the request goroutine, on the notification path (which runs on the read loop) and around a deferred follow-up, logs the panic with its stack, and answers `-32603 internal error in <method>: <panic>` — without it a bug in one handler closed the transport and the editor showed only `incoming_transport_closed` for the request that was in flight (`TestHandlerPanicAnswersAndKeepsServing`). stdin EOF closes every session's agent (stopping its MCP servers) and exits 0 with an empty stderr; SIGINT/SIGTERM cancel running prompts, then the same.

**Model check.** `session/new` validates the model *after* applying the server's `--model` flag and adding a local provider it names (`config.UseProvider`), and it runs `DiscoverOllama` first when `providers.ollama` is configured or the model is `ollama/…` — a running local model used to be reported as "unknown model" with an authentication error (F-2). The check's registry gets the default HTTP client like the agent's does: `Options.HTTP` is nil outside tests, and v0.7.8 handed that nil to discovery, so with `providers.ollama` configured every `session/new` and `authenticate` panicked into `-32603 internal error … nil pointer dereference` (`TestModelCheckWithOllamaConfigured`). The check's registry is the session's (`agent.OpenRegistry`, handed to `agent.Start` as `StartOptions.Registry`): `session/new` discovers once, where `agent.Start` used to build and discover a second registry — every provider whose list could not be read (a failure is never cached) paid its fetch timeout twice per session (`TestNewSessionDiscoversOnce`).

**Trust (per workdir).** `acpTrust` is `decideTrust` without the prompt: an explicit `--approve`/`--no-approve` wins; else yolo trusts; else a saved `trust.json` decision for that workdir is honoured (a "no" given in the TUI stays a no); else trusted (the editor opening the folder is the trust act). ACP never writes `trust.json`. Untrusted workdirs skip the project config layer, project instructions, project skills/prompts and the project's MCP servers (§10.5) — a trusted one gets all of them, project MCP servers included.

**Methods.** `initialize` → `{protocolVersion: 1, agentCapabilities: {loadSession, promptCapabilities {image: false, audio: false, embeddedContext: true}, mcpCapabilities {http: false, sse: false}, sessionCapabilities {list, resume, close, delete}}, authMethods: [{id: "login", name: "Sign in", description}], agentInfo {name: "moca", version}}` — exactly one method, of ACP's **default `agent` kind** (no `type` field), because the registry CI rejects an empty `authMethods` and an editor needs an affordance beside a session that cannot open (v0.7.8). `authenticate` accepts that id and answers by running the **same credential check** `session/new` makes (`checkModel`: registry + `CheckCredential`, ollama discovery included), returning `{}` on success and the same `-32603` fix on failure (no model configured included: the same "no model configured" message `session/new` gives, v0.7.10 — it used to surface `SplitModel`'s complaint about an empty model string) — so a sign-in button never reports success while no credential resolves; an id moca never advertised is `-32602`. It is not the `terminal` kind: ACP allows that only when the client enabled `clientCapabilities.auth.terminal`, and the client would then re-run the configured invocation interactively (`moca acp`) — a second ACP server, not a login (`moca login` needs a provider name as an argument, so it cannot be expressed as appended `args`). `session/new` refuses a missing model, an unknown one, or a provider with no usable credential as **`-32603`** carrying the fix (`checkModel` builds the registry and calls `CheckCredential` before the agent starts — the TUI's own `/model` gate — so the editor never gets a session whose every prompt fails). `-32603`, not ACP's auth-required: the fix is outside the editor (`moca login`, the config's `model`), so a `-32000` would replace the fix with a prompt that cannot complete it — the advertised method makes the affordance discoverable but cannot configure a model either, so the error still carries the fix as itself (v0.7.6, kept when the method landed in v0.7.8). `session/new` validates `cwd` (absolute, existing directory, else `-32602`), ignores `mcpServers` (count logged at debug; moca's own `mcp.servers` apply), starts the agent, answers `{sessionId, modes, configOptions}`, then sends `available_commands_update` (updates emitted during start are held until the response is written and flushed by the first request that names the session id, so that request's own notifications cannot land behind its response; v0.7.0: the list advertises `/remember` — hint `rule text` — ahead of the user templates only when self-managed skills are on for that session, never the project's auto skills; the startup `Note` about them reaches the client as a `notice` when the extension is advertised and as a thought line otherwise). `session/prompt`, `session/cancel`, `session/set_mode`, `session/set_config_option`, `session/list`, `session/load`, `session/resume`, `session/close`, `session/delete`, `$/cancel_request`.

**`session/prompt`.** Content blocks flatten in order into one user message: `text` verbatim; `resource_link` → `@<path>` (a `file://` URI becomes a path, relative to the workdir when inside it); embedded text `resource` → a fenced block headed by its URI; blob resources, `image` and `audio` → `-32602`. A leading `/name` expands a prompt template (`skills.ExpandPrompt`) or a skill (`skills.SkillMessage`, shared with the TUI), else the text is sent unchanged. One prompt per session at a time (a second is an error). `stopReason`: success `end_turn`; context cancelled (`session/cancel`, shutdown) `cancelled`; `Outcome.MaxSteps`/`Stuck` `max_turn_requests`; last turn `StopLength` `max_tokens`; `StopRefusal` `refusal`; any other error a `-32603` response. After the run moca reloads prompts and skills and re-sends `available_commands_update` if the list changed. `session/cancel` is a notification: it cancels the prompt's context; a pending permission request resolves to deny.

**Events → `session/update`.**

| `agent.Event` | update |
|---|---|
| `TextDelta` | `agent_message_chunk` `{content: {type: text}, messageId}` |
| `ThinkingDelta` | `agent_thought_chunk` |
| `StreamReset` | none; the next chunk uses a new `messageId` (sent chunks cannot be retracted) |
| `ToolStart` | `tool_call` `{toolCallId, name, title, kind, status: in_progress, rawInput, locations}` |
| `ToolEnd` | `tool_call_update` `{toolCallId, status: completed\|failed, content}`; a ToolEnd without a prior ToolStart (a cut-off turn's synthetic result) first sends a `tool_call` `in_progress` so the editor sees the call. A `write`/`edit` carries a `diff` content block, and **each side is capped at 8 KB** (`capHead`, with a "… more bytes not shown" marker) — uncapped, a 1 MB file became a 1 MB JSON-RPC frame per call (`TestEditUpdateCapsBothDiffSides`) |
| `ModeChanged`, `PlanChanged` | `current_mode_update` |
| `Warning` | `notice` (severity warning) when the client advertised `clientCapabilities.session.notices`, else an `agent_thought_chunk` prefixed `moca: ` |
| `Retry` | `notice` (info) when the client advertised `session.notices`; otherwise none |
| `TurnEnd` | `usage_update` (`used`, `size`, `cost`); the next chunk starts a new `messageId` |
| `Resumed`, `SteeringApplied` | none |

After each model turn moca sends `usage_update` (`used`, `size`, `cost`). Compaction sends `compaction_update` and `compaction_summary_chunk` only when the client advertised `clientCapabilities.session.compaction`; otherwise a live compaction shows as one `agent_thought_chunk` (`moca: context compacted (N → M tokens)`) and a compaction summary in the replayed history (`session/load`) as the one-line "_(earlier conversation was summarized)_" chunk. `session_info_update` carries the title once (the first line of the first prompt) and `updatedAt` after each prompt. After a prompt, `config_option_update` is sent when the config options changed.

**Tool calls.**

| Tool | `kind` | title | content on end |
|---|---|---|---|
| `read` | `read` | `read <path>` | `Result.Summary` only — never the file body |
| `write` | `edit` | `write <path>` | `diff {path, newText: <content>}` (`oldText` omitted — a new file) |
| `edit` | `edit` | `edit <path>` | `diff {path, oldText: old_string, newText: new_string}` |
| `shell` | `execute` | the command (first line, ≤ 80 chars) | output text, capped at 8 KB (tail kept) |
| `search` | `search` | `search <pattern>` | text, capped at 8 KB |
| `ls` | `search` | `ls <path>` | text, capped at 8 KB |
| `web` | `fetch` | `search <q>` / `fetch <url>` | text, capped at 8 KB |
| `mcp` | `other` | `mcp <server>/<tool>` (or the action) | text, capped at 8 KB |

`name` is the tool's programmatic name (`read`, `write`, `edit`, `shell`, `search`, `ls`, `web`, `mcp`), sent on the first report only — ACP: include it when available, never change it later (so `tool_call_update` omits it). `locations` is `[{path}]` (absolute, resolved against the workdir) when the input has a path. `Result.IsError` → `status: failed` with the error text. Inputs that do not parse fall back to the tool name as title with no locations. Tools read and write the disk directly; client `fs/*` and `terminal/*` are not used.

**Permissions (`session/request_permission`).** `toolCall {toolCallId: <current tool call>, title: "<Kind>: <Subject>", rawInput: {detail}}`; options `allow-once` ("Allow once", `allow_once`), `allow-session` ("Allow for this session", `allow_always` — the closest kind the schema has; v0.7.7, offered on every question that can be remembered, `rm` included; on the exact tier it reads "Allow this exact command for this session", the TUI row's wording, since v0.7.10) and `reject_once` ("Deny"). `selected` maps to allow once / allow for this session / deny; `cancelled`, an error response or a closed connection map to deny. ACP has never persisted an allow — the session answer is moca's in-memory one, keyed exactly as §11 states (a name where the rules could learn it, the exact command line where they could not) — and an excluded question (moca's own config file, a web fetch's redirect hop) offers no session option; an `allow-session` pick there is a client bug and answers deny. **Requests never time out:** `session/cancel` and stdin EOF are the only ways out — a timeout would silently deny under clients without auto-accept.

**Modes and config options.** `availableModes`: `ask`, `auto`, `yolo`, `plan` (`plan` = `SetMode(ask)` + `SetPlan(true)`; any other also clears plan; a client sending the legacy `default` is normalized to `ask`); the initial `currentModeId` is the resolved mode (`--mode` > trusted project `defaultMode` > config). `session/set_mode` and `session/set_config_option` are accepted only while idle (`Agent.SetMode`/`SetPlan`/`SetModel` are between-runs only) — mid-run they are an error ("wait for the current prompt to finish"). `configOptions` carries the same modes as a `mode` select (category `mode`; ACP's transition rule — an agent that offers mode-like configuration sends both APIs and a client that supports config options ignores `modes`, so the two are kept in sync: `session/set_mode` reports the changed option too, and setting the option also sends `current_mode_update`), then a `model` select (category `model`; `a.Models()` filtered by credential, the current model always included; `set_config_option` → `a.SetModel`), and an `effort` select (category `thought_level`) for models with at least two effort levels. An unknown `configId` or value is `-32602`.

**Sessions.** `session/list` — `session.List`, newest first, up to 100, no cursor; `cwd` optional (omitted = every workdir); sessions that never received a prompt are skipped; items `{sessionId, cwd, title, updatedAt}`. `session/load` — id8 only, the session's workdir must match `cwd`; the **full** history is replayed (user/assistant text, thinking, every tool call with its final status and content, a compaction summary as one message chunk) before the response, which carries `{modes, configOptions}`; a session already open in this process reuses its agent (a busy error — "wait for the current prompt to finish" — while a prompt runs). `session/resume` reopens without replay.
