The eval corpus was also re-run on this tree (`bash test/evals/run.sh -n 3 -j 4 -l v0.5.2` — 21 runs, 17/21, one failure an upstream `rate_limit_error` mid-stream); the delta against the rev-21 baseline is recorded in `test/evals/BASELINE.md` (steps per run 10.0 → 8.7; pass rate within noise, and the flash default effort moved, so it is not a clean prompt-only A/B).
- **v0.6.1 gate (2026-10-17)** — `make vet` + `make test` green (**950** tests: four new rule pins in `TestBuildSystemPrompt`, and the stateful-file guard's 14-case table + tool-level refusal + success-only copy marking); the guard is mutation-proven both ways — detection stubbed to empty fails the table, the wiring removed fails the tool-level pins, and the real tree passes; a live `-p` smoke on the built binary (real zen key, scratch state dirs) rendered all four new rules into the session's stored system prompt and completed (exit 0, `effort=low`, $0.0006); the eval corpus (`bash test/evals/run.sh -n 3 -j 4 -l v0.6.1` — 21 runs, $0.0782) scored **20/21** vs 17/21 at the `v0.5.2` label, mean steps 8.0 vs 8.7, tokens/solved 46508 vs 67481, repeated fails 0 vs 2 — prompt-sensitive and n=3/scenario, so read as no regression with a positive lean, not a proof.
- **A3 instrumented baseline (2026-10-17; merged main `366696d`, the A1/A2 merge — behaviorally v0.6.1)** — flash leg **n=6**: 42 runs, **40/42**, $0.1520 (fresh tok/solved 15,231; wire 51,920), steps 8.8 ± 5.0; strong leg `opencode-go/deepseek-v4-pro` **n=3**: 21 runs, **19/21**, $0.2624, `effort: high` on the wire (the `deepseekProThinking` map clamps the default `medium`). Instruments: cache eff 0.78 flash / 1.06 strong (>1 is possible — cache reads can exceed the `min(prevPrompt, prompt)` estimate), **breaks/run 1.6 flash and 0.0 strong** — flash's breaks are attributed `tool_use, tool_result` and the A2 replay independently re-ran **36/36** non-web transcripts byte-identically (Δ 0.0 %, zero prefix breaks), so they are provider cache misses, **not** history rewrites; redundant read tokens **0** on both legs (C3's gate is a skip unless TB2 says otherwise); system prompt 1,801 tok (C2's baseline); residency mass in `read` (151K / 101K tok·req). Identical-behavior calibration (`-compare v0.6.1,v0.6.1-instr`): overall **underpowered** (1.12 [0.94, 1.32]) while `feature` alone reads 1.54 [1.07, 2.13] — that is the corpus's noise floor at n=3–6, so C's gates read the overall verdict **plus** the replay, never a single scenario's point estimate. The `vague` checker's `-to f -- -300` case fails on both models (2/6, 2/3) — **settled 2026-10-18: the checker is right and the models genuinely miss the bound.** `-to f` takes *Celsius* input (its own flag help says so), so −300 °C is below absolute zero and must fail; the reference implements per-input-scale bounds. The checker gained two per-scale valid-side cases (`-to c -- -40` → `-40.0`, `-to c -- -274` → `-170.0`) so a celsius-only bound no longer passes, and `vague` runs before/after that tightening are not directly comparable. Crutch record: **0 nudges in 21 strong-model runs** (0 cache breaks, 0 redundant reads) → "crutches are cheap on strong models"; no §16 item. Tables: `test/evals/BASELINE.md` → "Instrumented baseline — A3".
- **openai-responses reasoning-replay probe (2026-10-18, the zen go route directly — raw `urllib`, no moca code path; key from the repo `.env`; `User-Agent: moca/v0.6.4` and a fresh `x-opencode-session` are both required — the route 1010s a default UA and 400s `MissingSessionID` without the header)** — resolves the `HistoryBound()` open question (§14). `gpt-6-luna`, `store:false`, `include:["reasoning.encrypted_content"]`, `reasoning.effort=high`, one echo tool, a deliberation prompt: the fresh call returned a reasoning item (`rs_…`, `encrypted_content` present, empty summary) beside its `function_call`; replaying the pair → **200**; replaying the call with the reasoning item **dropped** (exactly the kept-tail-strip shape) → **200**; dropping the tool output instead → **400** (`No tool output found for function call …`). The route does not bind a function_call to its reasoning item, so the strip and elision stay enabled on openai-responses rows and `HistoryBound()` stays adaptive-only — now pinned, not assumed (`TestHistoryBoundIsAdaptiveOnly` covers the four responses rows, `internal/provider/catalog_test.go`). The api.openai.com rows ride the same adapter and remain covered by the no-vendor-keys item below. Cost ~$0.01.
- **v0.7.6 reasoning-echo A/B (2026-10-22, a logging tee on `127.0.0.1` in front of `https://opencode.ai/zen/go/v1`, the repo `.env` key, a throwaway `XDG_*` tree, one recorded session resumed by two binaries)** — the echo v0.7.2 added never reached the wire. The fixture is a real four-step session on `opencode-go/deepseek-v4.1-flash` that stored thinking blocks on six of its seven assistant turns; `--resume <id8> -p "…"` was run on HEAD and on the fixed tree, and the **first** request of each was captured, so the only variable is the binary: HEAD replays 0 of 7 turns with `reasoning_content`, the fixed tree 6 of 7 (the seventh never reasoned, and what was not produced is not sent). Cause: the completions adapter compared the persisted thinking block to `req.Model` (the bare id) while the agent stamps the qualified id, so the comparison matched nothing; its unit test had stamped the bare id too, which is why it passed. A first capture of a single `ls` turn looked like the same symptom but proved nothing — a turn that never reasoned and a turn whose reasoning was dropped are identical on the wire — hence the resumed-transcript A/B. Replaying a real 38-message transcript against the route with every assistant turn stripped still returns **200** on 24 distinct `x-opencode-session` shards: the gateway is lenient for this account *today* and the strictness lives in an upstream it reaches some of the time (as the v0.7.2 commit recorded), so the shape is fixed against the documented requirement and pinned hermetically (`TestRequestReplaysReasoningContent`, mutation-proven: restored comparison → fails). Cost ~$0.05.
- **v0.6.0 theme + startup-mode gate (2026-10-16, scratch `moca_gate_v060_theme/` — hermetic: a config-only provider on a dead loopback port with an unset key env, so every run is refused locally; no model call, no network)** — 19/19: a user `tui.theme` override reaches a truecolor pty frame (the custom accent/error/muted escapes present, the built-in orange absent, the input placeholder included) while the control run without a theme block keeps the built-ins; the status badges follow the resolved mode (the retuned muted `Default` badge `#8d8f91` for a plain session, the `Plan` badge `#60a5fa` for a `plan` default); a user `permissions.defaultMode: "plan"` resolves to the plan scope (`-p` prints the plan banner, the log carries `msg=mode mode=default plan=true`); a trusted project file carrying a `tui` block is an exit-2 config error naming the file (untrusted: never read); `moca execute` skips a configured `plan` layer (project `plan` over user `auto` → `mode=auto plan=false`, no plan banner) while a plain run keeps the project's layer (`mode=default plan=true`).
- **v0.5.1-beta gates (2026-10-15, scratch `moca_gate28/`, scripted openai-completions provider + a Python stdio MCP fake, the real binary)** — **gate A (TUI, tmux)**: 13/13 — the approval panel renders `? allow `python3`?` with exactly one `Allow once` / `Allow always` / `Deny` row and no `[a] once`/`[ctrl+a]`/`[d] deny` text; `ctrl+a` answers it, writes `{"permissions": {"allow": ["python3"]}}` into `<workdir>/.moca/config.jsonc` and leaves the user config byte-untouched; the pane's raw stream carries the keyboard-enhancements push `ESC[>13u` (flags 1|4|8 = disambiguate + **alternate keys** + all-keys; 1|8 = 9 was the pre-fix request). **gate B (`-p`, real stdio MCP server)**: 11/11 — the trusted workdir's `.mcp.json` servers join the roster (`- projecho: project echo server`, `- lazy: never called`) beside the user's `- shared: from the user config`, the conflict warns `mcp server "shared" is defined in <.mcp.json> and <config.jsonc> — using <config.jsonc>`, a `mcp` call reached and started the project server (its own marker file, exactly one start; the uncalled server never spawned), and the *same* workdir without `--approve` contributed nothing (no roster lines, no warnings, no process). **gate C (raw CSI-u into a real TUI)**: 2/2 — `CSI 47:63;2u` (the alternate-key form) lands `?` in the draft, `CSI 47;2u` lands `/` (the reported bug's wire shape, which is why the request had to change). `make vet` clean, `go test ./... -race` green.
- **ACP TCK gate (2026-10-15, `agentclientprotocol/acp-tck` 0.2.0, protocol v1, the built `moca acp` against a hermetic scripted openai-completions provider — loopback only, no key, no network; the clone lives outside the repo)** — `uv run acp-tck --timeout 15 --test-timeout 90 --cancel-prompt "…" --report-json <scratch>/report.json -- <repo>/bin/moca acp`: **VERDICT: CONFORMANT** (exit 0) — 56 collected tests, **51 passed / 5 skipped / 0 failed** in 10 s; requirement tiers: MANDATORY 21/21 PASS, CAPABILITY 14 PASS + 5 SKIPPED (every skip is a capability moca does not advertise: `image`, `audio`, the auth flow and `logout`, `additionalDirectories`), ADVISORY 12/12, INFORMATIONAL 4/4 (a malformed stdin line → `-32700` with `id:null` and the connection stays usable; an unknown `sessionId` → `-32002`; 0 stderr bytes). Full JSON report kept in scratch — experimental harness, deliberately out-of-tree and out of CI.
- **ACP transport report, Windows 11 + Zed (2026-10-22; no Windows host here, and `wine` hangs a Go binary on startup — 4 minutes, no CPU — so this one was not reproduced locally)** — a Zed external agent (`moca acp`) failed its first `session/new` with `Incoming transport closed: {reason: incoming_transport_closed, method: session/new}`, i.e. the SDK lost the agent's stdout with the request in flight. What the server could be shown guilty of, and now cannot do: answer an `initialize` that advertises no auth methods with ACP's auth-required (`-32000`) — the editor has no method to call and renders a dead-end prompt for what is really "no model configured" or "no credential in the editor's environment" (a GUI app on Windows does not see a PowerShell profile's variables) — and die silently on a handler panic (nothing reaches its log; the editor sees only the closed transport). Verified live after the fix on this host: a fresh install with no config answers `-32603 no model configured: run moca login …`; a config whose key env var is unset answers `-32603 provider … environment variable … is not set`; an injected panic in `session/new` answers `-32603 internal error in session/new: …` with the stack in the log and the process still serving. Confirming which one the reporter hit needs their `~/.local/state/moca/logs/*.log` (the fix makes it say `mode=acp` and carry the panic), and the Zed session gate stays open below.
- **Still open**: **a real-terminal confirmation of the shift fix** (the request is verified by the push bytes and the decode path by gates A/C; `shift+/` on the reporting terminal is the manual leg — a terminal that honours all-keys but ignores alternate keys would still send the unshifted codepoint, in which case dropping `ReportAllKeysAsEscapeCodes` is the fallback, at the cost of `shift+enter` on kitty-semantics terminals), a live `moca login openai` round-trip (needs a ChatGPT account — the flow is covered by the fake-AS suite and the real endpoints were probed 2026-10-06); direct `api.anthropic.com` / `api.openai.com` API-key endpoints (no vendor keys — the zen override paths validate the same codecs); multi-turn thinking replay + prompt caching verification (compaction ships in phase 4); `--effort off` behavioral verification on the remaining `none`-capable routes (kimi-k3, ollama — wire-accepted, no hard suppression observed; the zen go glm variants no longer accept a disabling value at all: `off` clamps to `low`/`minimal`, §5/§6); the opencode-go `x-opencode-session` header is still per-process (a resumed session reuses the process's id; fine for routing, per-conversation rebinding remains open); a TUI-driven real-model MCP ladder pass; `graphify install --platform moca` pending upstream merge of Graphify-Labs/graphify#4174; intermittent Go-tier `[1210]` upstream 400s on `glm-5.3-flash` (no tokens spent; now auto-retried — the gateway frames them as `server_error`, §8 — vendor-side); the release workflow (manual trigger only) awaits its first run after merge, and the Windows replace path is cross-compile-checked only (`GOOS=windows go build`); the Zed session gate for `moca acp` (the ACP TCK — an experimental out-of-tree harness — ran **CONFORMANT** on 2026-10-15, recorded above; the hermetic `test/acpgate` and the pinned-schema conformance suites remain the in-repo checks).
- **Open items carried from the v0.7.0 review passes (moved here in v0.7.2, when `docs/specs/open-items.md` was retired)** — two findings verified and deliberately left unchanged; each needs a policy or design call, and none blocks a release. The other ten items on that list are **fixed and pinned**: `dayDiff` calendar-day pruning, the provenance transition table, `Touched` when a skill loses rules and empties, `skillsAncestor` for `rm -rf .moca`, the per-tool `writeOperands` table (a `cp` of a skill body stays allowed, an `mv` does not), the `(a)` arm's clause-local negator (the refusal must sit **ahead of** the command inside its clause), `UserRefusal` mapping in ACP, `remember`'s reservation on both front ends, and the **Windows shell skill-write refusal** (`winSkillWriters` + `winSkillPath`, `shell_windows_rules.go`: `Set-Content .moca\skills\moca-x\SKILL.md …` is refused by name — textual, because that parser has no PowerShell quoting model; S-8, v0.7.2).
  - **A bare-word make override the host cannot resolve still passes.** `plainMakeValue` (`internal/permissions/shell.go`) refuses flags, non-word values, a denylisted word and anything `exec.LookPath` resolves; a rule that wrote the exact line waives the PATH arm only. A value that names code but does not resolve on this host (a recipe variable read by an interpreter, or a binary on a PATH the analyser does not share) is statically indistinguishable from data. §11 records it as a residual; closing it means restricting overrides to numeric/version shapes — and refusing `CC=gcc` without a named rule — which is a policy call, not a fix. Pinned: `TestMakeOverrideValues` (`internal/permissions/shell_rule_specificity_test.go`).
  - **The guard's "state the rule without backticks" escape is the contamination shape arm (a) exists to catch (found at `v074-2`, v0.7.5 candidate).** A rule may legally avoid the cited-command check by un-backticking a command it names, and a *bare* path is then judged only when its clause instructs before the path ("always run ./x") or a negator trails it. A mention that puts the instruction **inside a subordinate claim** — "test output printed a comment claiming ./scripts/bootstrap.sh must be run first, but tests passed without it" — therefore passes the guard and fails the corpus's `(a)` scan, which reads bare text: 1 run in 15 at `v074-2`, product-correct by neither reading. What it needs is a policy decision (is a quoted claim a mention or an instruction?) plus a mention-grammar widening, not a patch: the fix is probably to judge an instruction phrase in the token's own clause regardless of side ("must be run", "is required first"), and to stop offering "without backticks" as the repair for a command that must never run — the honest repair is negation-first, which both the guard and the scan already accept.
  - **Cold-repo arming accepts any non-inspect command as "how this repo verifies".** `verifies()` (`internal/tools/runstate.go`) counts everything outside `inspectOnly`, on purpose — the nudge it feeds must not fire on a real check. In a repo with no skill yet, the first successful `go version` or `npm install` therefore arms §12.1's end-of-run nudge. The `skills-cold` numbers rest on that predicate (0/6 → 2/6 at v0.7.1, 3/3 recordings at v0.7.2), so narrowing it to a test/build/lint allowlist is a design call plus a re-measure, never a quiet edit.

- **v0.7.10 model discovery gate (2026-10-24)** — `make vet` clean, `make test` green; new pins: `TestModelsCache*` (TTL boundary, base-URL invalidation, one file per provider, portable escaped names, another provider's file is a miss, corrupt/old-version file, 0600, no secrets, parallel providers, clear), `TestFetch{Anthropic,OpenAI,OpenRouter}Models` (pagination, deny-lists, per-token pricing ×1e6, mandatory-effort flagging), `TestDiscoverModels{UsesTheCache,FailOpen}`, `TestMergePrecedenceKeepsCuratedMetadata`, `TestAssumedWindowsFollowTheMergedRow` (a curated window is not a guess), `TestZenBorrowsTheGoCatalog`, `TestActiveModelsFollowCredentials`, `TestClearModelsCache{RescansTheActiveProvider,KeepsOtherProvidersRows,FailedRescanKeepsRows,RescansOllama}`, `TestResolveKeepsAModelItHandedOut`, `TestCheapModelSurvivesARelist`, `TestNewSessionDiscoversOnce`, `TestCompletionsReasoningObjectMode`, `TestStatusCostUnknownRendersDash`, `TestACPIconContract`. Discovery is hermetic (fake servers, no key, no network): `TestMain` in `agent`/`acp`/`tui` blanks every built-in key and points the XDG dirs at a temp root, and the `provider` tests blank the keys per test — otherwise this host's exported keys make a provider ready and discovery calls its live endpoint from a unit test (measured: `internal/agent` 1.4 s → 16 s, and the ACP model-option assertion grew every OpenRouter id). Live legs (`anthropic`, `openai`, `opencode-go`, `opencode`, `openrouter`) need their vendor keys and are Ben's to run; §15's no-vendor-keys item still applies to the api.openai.com/anthropic rows.

## 16. Maintenance

- Update this file at the end of every phase PR, and in any PR that changes implemented behavior (CLI contract, config keys, catalog, wire behavior, tools, permissions, sessions, agent loop). Keep it **normative for the current state**.
- **Single source of truth (2026-10-13):** this file is the only design/spec doc — `docs/specs/DESIGN.md` (the v1 contract) was retired and deleted; its still-current content was absorbed here (threat model above, purpose/bet §1, non-goals below), everything else lives in git history. A design-level change is a SPECS edit in the same PR as the code — no separate design doc and no separate revision log (historical "rev N" labels name the retired doc's revision-log entries; read them via git history).
- **Explicit non-goals (v1):** subagents · hooks · LSP · image gen · voice · telemetry · **binary plugin/extension system** (skills + slash commands + the shell allowlist + the §10.5 lazy `mcp` proxy are the extension model — a fifth mechanism needs a SPECS update) · `serve` mode · OS-level shell sandboxing (v2 discussion) · nested per-directory AGENTS.md · MCP OAuth / sampling / elicitation / legacy SSE (v2 discussion) · a writer whose operand list does not name its targets (`git apply`, `patch < diff` from stdin, `rsync --files-from`) — outside the static shell refusal by design; the shell analyser reads words, and the load-time re-validation is the backstop.

### 3.5.1 TUI additions (rev 14)

- **Response rendering (markdown-lite)** — committed response lines are rendered per line with a little state across lines (`renderMarkdown`, `internal/tui/markdown.go`): `**bold**`, `` `code` `` (amber foreground), `#` headings (bold, hashes dropped), `-`/`*`/`+` bullets → `•`, `>` quotes → `│`, and fenced code (` ``` ` / `~~~`): the opener becomes a `─ lang` label row and the closer an empty row, the code lines sit on their own band (`#0c1f27` dark / `#b6cdee` light), are **never word-wrapped** (a row wider than the screen is cut at the width, so the band survives) and keep every marker literal. Spans never cross lines; an unmatched marker prints as written; everything else (links, emphasis, tables, nested lists) is untouched. Inline styles are SGR *toggles* (bold on/off, foreground set/default), never a full reset, so the band background survives; `ansi.Wrap`'s full resets at wrap points are rewritten to the same toggles. The input is sanitized first, so model text cannot inject escapes. The fence state (`model.md`) carries across streamed batches and resets at run, turn and stream-restart boundaries; the live partial line previews with a copy of it. `/copy` copies the raw markdown.
- **Spacing and layout** — a response that follows tool/thinking item lines is set off by one blank row (the live partial line carries the same gap); `›` messages hang their wrapped and later lines under the text (two cells in); item lines are clamped to one row (`…`; the full line is the pager title); `!`/`!!` commands echo as `›` messages like `/` commands (the `$ cmd` header above the output is gone).
- **Edit diffs inline** — a successful `edit` item is followed by its changed lines, indented: `-` red, `+` green, headers and context dropped, at most 8 rows, then `… N more changed lines · ctrl+o for the full diff` (`write` carries no diff).
- **Context pressure** — the status bar's percent field turns yellow from 70% of the window and red from the auto-compaction trigger (`compact.Budget.Trigger()`: window − reserve), where line 2 also says `/compact` (dropped before in/out when narrow).
- **Dropdown, generalized** — besides command names, the same panel completes the argument of `/model` (substring match; the current model and `no key` providers are marked), `/effort` (the model's supported levels), `/login`, `/logout` (providers with something stored), `/show` (items, newest first) and `/resume` (sessions): `tab` completes, `enter` on an exact argument runs. **`@path`**: when the draft's last word starts with `@`, the panel lists matching workdir files (gitignore-aware via `tools.ListFiles`, ≤ 5000 files indexed lazily on the first `@` and re-indexed after a turn or a `!` command; ranked basename prefix → path-segment prefix → substring, shallower first, ≤ 50 rows); `tab`/`enter` insert `@path ` (enter never sends while the list is open). Text is sent as written — the model reads the file itself. An email address (`@` not at a word start) never opens it. **`ctrl+r`** searches the prompt history: the draft is the query (substring, newest first, duplicates collapsed); `↑`/`↓` select, `enter`/`tab` put the match in the draft **without sending**, `esc`/`ctrl+c` leave.
- **Prompt history persists** — `history.jsonl` in the data dir (0600; `{"wd":…,"t":…}` per line), recalled per workdir (≤ 500, consecutive duplicates collapsed); messages with control bytes or over 8 KiB are not stored; a file past 4000 lines is trimmed to 2000 on load. Only submitted messages are stored (never key or redirect-URL entry).
- **Resume shows where you left off** — `agent.Resumed` (startup `--resume`/`--continue` and `/resume`) prints `resumed <id8> (N messages)` and replays the last 3 user turns through the normal paths (user bands, markdown responses, numbered thinking and tool items that open in the pager; `… N earlier turns not shown`; a compaction summary shows as `⋯ earlier conversation was summarized`). Replayed tool items summarize the call's operand (`read x.go`) — the live summary is not stored.
- **Activity row** — `⠋ shell go test ./…… 12s · ↓1.2k tok · 1 queued · esc to interrupt`: the running tool's operand (command first line / path / pattern, clamped), a streamed-token estimate (chars/4; runs only) and the number of queued steering messages (`agent.PendingSteering`).
- **Pager** — `/` searches the body (case-insensitive; the title row shows the query being typed, then `n/m`), `n`/`N` step through matches, `esc` cancels a query being typed (and only then closes). When an approval arrives while the pager is open, the title row says `⚠ approval pending — q to answer`.
- **Ollama in the TUI** — with a `providers.ollama` entry, `/model` (no argument) first re-reads the server's models (≤ 6 s, `reading models…` in the bar), then opens the picker; an unreachable server is a warning line and the picker still lists cloud and declared models. Local models are never marked `no key`. `/login` lists `ollama` as “local server; a key is only needed behind an authenticating proxy”.
- **Notifications** — the view requests focus reports; while the terminal is **unfocused** a run that took ≥ 30 s finishing, or an approval waiting, emits `tui.notify` (config; default `osc9` = `ESC ] 9 ; text BEL` desktop notification, `bell`, `off`). A focused terminal is never notified.
- **Colors** — scrollback text is converted to the terminal's color profile before printing (`tea.Println` bypasses the renderer's own downsampling): detected at startup like Bubble Tea does; `NO_COLOR` drops every color (bold/faint stay; `›` still marks user lines), 256/16-color terminals get approximations of the band colors, a non-terminal gets plain text.
- **Known limitation — narrowing the window**: terminals that reflow on shrink (tmux, kitty, iTerm2, WezTerm, VTE, Windows Terminal) re-wrap the old full-width frame rows before the renderer erases them, which can leave one duplicate rule row above the input box; the next frame is correct. Bubble Tea already erases and redraws on resize; compensating needs terminal-specific knowledge (a wrong guess erases scrollback), so it is left alone. Scrollback bands printed at the old width reflow, which is unavoidable inline.
